<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that deciding whether Weisfeiler–Leman refinement of an input dimension identifies a given graph is EXPTIME-complete. The input is a nonempty finite simple uncolored graph in adjacency-matrix form and a positive binary-encoded dimension. Identification quantifies over every comparison graph.

- **C1** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The following problem is EXPTIME-complete under deterministic polynomial-time many-one reductions: given a nonempty finite simple undirected uncolored graph $G$ by its adjacency matrix and a positive integer $k$ in binary, decide whether $\operatorname{WLdim}(G)\le k$. Here dimension one uses ordinary neighbor color refinement, and dimensions at least two use the joint-update rule described above.
  - Explanation: Classifies the identification threshold problem with the dimension supplied as input.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-wl-game)): Let $G,H$ have equal positive order, and let $k\ge 2$. Then $G\equiv_k H$ if and only if Duplicator has a winning strategy from the empty position in the bijective game with $k+1$ pair slots. Moreover, a winning strategy with at least two pair slots implies $G\equiv_1 H$.
  - Explanation: Relates joint-vector refinement to a winning bijective game strategy and ordinary neighbor refinement.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-monotonicity)): For positive integers $j\le k$, the relation $G\equiv_k H$ implies $G\equiv_j H$. Consequently, a graph has Weisfeiler–Leman dimension at most $k$ if and only if $k$-WL identifies it.
  - Explanation: Makes identification at the supplied dimension equivalent to the dimension threshold.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-degree)): In every history following a winning strategy with at least two pair slots, matched vertices have equal degrees. Every vertex bijection announced by that strategy therefore preserves degrees for all its possible choices.
  - Explanation: Preserves degrees under every announced bijection in a winning strategy.
- **C5** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-upper)): For a nonempty graph given by its binary adjacency matrix and a positive integer $k$ in binary, deciding whether its Weisfeiler–Leman dimension is at most $k$ belongs to EXPTIME.
  - Explanation: Decides identification by enumerating comparison graphs and testing stabilized refinement and isomorphism.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-mate)): For any comparison data just described, with $r\ge 32$, every graph $H$ satisfying $H\equiv_r G(0)$ is isomorphic to $G(\beta)$ for some choice of one bit $\beta_B$ per square block.
  - Explanation: Recovers an arbitrary equivalent mate as a graph with shifted square constraints.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-isomorphism)): For every choice of square bits $\beta$, the graphs $G(0)$ and $G(\beta)$ are isomorphic if and only if there is a global offset assignment solving all shifted square constraints.
  - Explanation: Characterizes graph isomorphism by a single compatible assignment to all shared readouts.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-image-consistency)): For the construction of Section <a href="#sec:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:encoding">3</a>, suppose $G(0)\equiv_r G(\beta)$. There are nonempty sets $Q_{P,a}\subseteq\mathbb F_2^{d_P}$ for every real type $P$ and every $a\in\{0,1\}^r$ such that, for every comparison $e:P\to R$, $$\lambda_{e,0}(Q_{P,a}) =\Delta_e(a)+\lambda_{e,1}(Q_{R,\sigma_e(a)}) \qquad(a\in D_e). \label{eq:image-consistency}$$ Here the equality concerns scalar image sets for one comparison at a time, not a simultaneous choice of vectors for all comparisons.
  - Explanation: Extracts nonempty scalar-image consistent sets without requiring a simultaneous vector choice.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cycle-lifting)): Let $r\ge3$, let each $A_i$ be nonempty, and let $g_i:A_i\times A_{i+1}\to\mathbb F_2$. If $$\sum_{i=1}^r g_i(a_i,a_{i+1})=0 \qquad\text{for all }a\in\prod_i A_i, \label{eq:zero-cycle-sum}$$ then there are functions $Y_i:A_i\to\mathbb F_2$ with $g_i(u,v)=Y_i(u)+Y_{i+1}(v)$ for every $i,u,v$.
  - Explanation: Factors an identically vanishing cycle sum into consecutive corner potentials.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-global-offsets)): For any right-hand sides $\beta$ in the construction of Section <a href="#sec:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:encoding">3</a>, all square equations admit a simultaneous assignment to the readout names if and only if there are row-sum functions $h_P:\{0,1\}^r\to\mathbb F_2^{d_P}$, one for each real type, satisfying $$\lambda_{e,0}(h_P(a))+\lambda_{e,1}(h_R(\sigma_e(a))) =\Delta_e(a) \qquad(e:P\to R,\ a\in D_e). \label{eq:global-offsets}$$ Equivalently, these functions exist exactly when $G(0)\cong G(\beta)$.
  - Explanation: Characterizes simultaneous square solvability using adjacent-coordinate row-sum functions.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-special-twist)): Consider the graph construction with $r\geq32$ and $K=r+1$. Let $\beta$ be given by Equation <a href="#eq:special-twist" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:special-twist">[eq:special-twist]</a>. Suppose that, for every real type $P$ and every $a\in\{0,1\}^r$, there is a nonempty set $Q_{P,a}\subseteq\mathbb F_2^{d_P}$, and that these sets satisfy $$\lambda_{e,0}(Q_{P,a}) =b_e+\lambda_{e,1}(Q_{R,\sigma_e(a)}) \qquad(e:P\longrightarrow R,\ a\in D_e). \label{eq:special-images}$$ Then $G(0)\equiv_r G(\beta)$.
  - Explanation: Makes scalar-image consistency sufficient for equivalence under the specified concentrated twists.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-type-support)): If $\|U\|\leq K$, every component of $\Gamma_U$ has a type support that is a tree with at most one real type. A support containing no real type lies in a single wire interior. A support containing a real type consists of that type and short arms in its incident wires.
  - Explanation: Keeps the support of a small block component within a tree containing at most one real type.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-wrapping-address)): Let $\|U\|\leq r+1$.* 1. *Every wrapping subcollection of $U$ has at least $r$ blocks.* 2. *A wrapping collection of exactly $r$ blocks has one block in each row, lies in one component, and determines a full address in that component’s aligned labels.* 3. *All wrapping $r$-subcollections of $U$, if any, lie in one component of $\Gamma_U$ and determine the same aligned address there.
  - Explanation: Bounds wrapping collections and makes their aligned address unique within the stated collection size.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-exact-projection)): For $V\subseteq U$ with $\|U\|\leq r+1$, restriction to the readout names occurring in $V$ maps $\mathcal H_U$ onto $\mathcal H_V$.
  - Explanation: Extends every permitted old readout assignment to the larger block collection.
- **C15** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-computation)): For the fixed machine $M$ and each input $w$, with parameters <a href="#eq:computation-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:computation-parameters">[eq:computation-parameters]</a>, one can construct in time polynomial in $r+m$ an acyclic monotone circuit description with $O_M(1)$ gate types and $O_M((r+1)^2+m)$ connection schemas, seed requests, and test requests. Every schema has a nonempty product domain and coordinate injections; the whole description contains $O_M(r((r+1)^2+m))$ constant-size coordinate entries. All tests are scalar terminal gates. The machine $M$ accepts $w$ if and only if at least one designated test gate is true.
  - Explanation: Compiles the fixed machine computation into a polynomial-size description of an acyclic monotone circuit.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-acceptance)): If $M$ accepts $w$, then every finite simple uncolored graph $H\equiv_r G(0)$ is isomorphic to $G(0)$.
  - Explanation: Uses acceptance to make every equivalent mate isomorphic to the homogeneous graph.
- **C17** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-prop-rejection)): If $M$ does not accept $w$, then there is a choice of square bits $\beta$ such that $G(\beta)\equiv_r G(0)$ but $G(\beta)\not\cong G(0)$.
  - Explanation: Uses nonacceptance to construct an equivalent mate with contradictory global offset requirements.
- **C18** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-unary)): The identification problem of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> remains EXPTIME-complete when the positive dimension is encoded in unary.
  - Explanation: Preserves the complexity classification when the dimension is encoded in unary.

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-offsets",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-type-support",
        "exact_quote": "If $|U|\\leq K$, every component of $\\Gamma_U$ has a type support that is a tree with at most one real type. A support containing no real type lies in a single wire interior. A support containing a real type consists of that type and short arms in its incident wires.",

      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **WL equivalence** ([source](#eq:wl-update)): Write $G\equiv_k H$ if, at every round, the multisets of colors of their $k$-tuples agree.
- **WL identification** ([source](#eq:wl-update)): We say that $k$-WL *identifies* $G$ if $G\equiv_k H$ implies $G\cong H$ for every graph $H$.
- **Weisfeiler–Leman dimension** ([source](#eq:wl-update)): The Weisfeiler–Leman dimension of $G$ is the least positive $k$ that identifies it.
- **Row-sum form** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): For a nonempty product $A=\prod_{i=1}^r A_i$ and a vector space $V$ over $\mathbb F_2$, a function $h:A\to V$ has *row-sum form* if there are functions $h_i:A_i\times A_{i+1}\to V$ such that $$h(a)=\sum_{i=1}^r h_i(a_i,a_{i+1}). \label{eq:row-sum}$$ Indices are cyclic, so $A_{r+1}=A_1$ and $a_{r+1}=a_1$.
- **Global offset assignment** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A *global offset assignment* gives a vector in $\mathbb F_2^{d_t}$ to every horizontal name $x_{i,t,p,p'}$ and a scalar in $\mathbb F_2$ to every vertical name $y_{i,\ell,p,q}$, with the same value used in all blocks containing that name.
- **Wire shifts** ([source](#eq:wire-shift)): Define $$\Delta_\ell(a)=\sum_{i=1}^r\beta_{B(i,\ell,a)}, \qquad \Delta_e(a)=\sum_{\ell\text{ on }e}\Delta_\ell(a). \label{eq:wire-shift}$$
- **Wrapping** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A block collection, or a component of its corner graph, is *wrapping* if its corner graph has a closed walk whose marker sum in $\mathbb F_2$ is one.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-wl-game) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wl-game): Stable joint replacement colors provide the vertex bijections used by the game. -> Game characterization
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-monotonicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-monotonicity): Restrict winning strategies to fewer slots. -> Identification threshold
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-upper): Enumerate equal-order comparison graphs, stabilize common color tables, and test all vertex permutations. -> Exponential-time membership
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-mate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mate): Recover private leaves and base colors by degree intervals, then query complete small incidence pieces. -> Shifted description of every mate
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-isomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-isomorphism): Translate shared bit pairs and legal valuations by compatible offsets. -> Isomorphism criterion
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-image-consistency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-image-consistency): Transfer site-ring sums across wires while maintaining consistency on shared readouts. -> Scalar-image consistent sets
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-cycle-lifting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cycle-lifting): Use rectangular differences and a cyclic recursion for corner constants. -> Corner potentials
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-offsets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-offsets): Choose row decompositions and lift each link residual into vertical potentials. -> Global compatible offsets
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-exact-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exact-projection): Retain wrapping choices and compensate changed nonwrapping shifts with corner potentials. -> Surjective readout restriction
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-special-twist) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exact-projection-2): Choose an extension separately for each possible next owner and assemble its translations into a vertex bijection. -> Equivalence for special twists
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-computation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-computation): Apply the product-circuit compilation result and encode carry and borrow cases by coordinate injections. -> Polynomial-size circuit description
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-prop-acceptance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-acceptance): Propagate singleton images through private buffers, broadcast across addresses, and derive row-sum functions from self-connections. -> Every equivalent mate is isomorphic
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-prop-rejection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rejection): Twist one broadcast self-connection and construct correlated buffer sets while global offsets contradict each other. -> Equivalent nonisomorphic mate
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rejection-2): Combine exponential-time membership with the polynomial reduction and its acceptance equivalence. -> Complexity classification
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-cor-unary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-unary): Write the polynomially bounded output dimension in unary. -> Unary-encoding classification

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-identification**: Identification requires isomorphism of every equivalent comparison graph.
- **I-cycle**: An identically vanishing cyclic sum of adjacent-coordinate binary functions admits corner potentials.
- **I-global**: Graph isomorphism requires one simultaneous global offset assignment.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-pair**: Treat equivalence of a supplied graph pair as identification. -> The universal comparison-graph requirement is left unchecked.
- **A-images**: Use scalar image agreement as a global assignment certificate. -> Different comparisons can use incompatible supporting vectors.
- **A-convention**: Replace the joint update with separate coordinate histograms. -> The refinement convention changes.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Cycle lifting constructs corner potentials for finite adjacent-coordinate binary tables with identically vanishing cyclic sum.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of {sizes, tables}; row tables are rectangular binary arrays.
# PRECONDITIONS: r>=3; nonempty finite factors; binary entries; cyclic sum zero on every address.
# OUTPUTS: JSON batch of corner potentials, or rejection of failed preconditions.
# TIME: O(r*product(sizes)+sum adjacent table sizes) per instance.
# SPACE: O(sum adjacent table sizes+sum sizes+r).
# ARITHMETIC: exact binary XOR; certified integer summation for precondition checks.
# FAILURE: invalid dimensions or tables return invalid; nonzero cycle sum returns nonzero.
import json, sys, itertools
from flint import fmpz
def lift(item):
    sizes, tables = item['sizes'], item['tables']
    r = len(sizes)
    if r < 3 or any(type(n) is not int or n < 1 for n in sizes) or len(tables) != r:
        return {'status':'invalid'}
    for i, table in enumerate(tables):
        if len(table) != sizes[i] or any(len(row) != sizes[(i+1)%r] for row in table):
            return {'status':'invalid'}
        if any(type(bit) is not int or bit not in (0,1) for row in table for bit in row):
            return {'status':'invalid'}
    for address in itertools.product(*(range(n) for n in sizes)):
        if sum((fmpz(tables[i][address[i]][address[(i+1)%r]]) for i in range(r)), fmpz(0)) % 2:
            return {'status':'nonzero'}
    constants = [tables[i][0][0] for i in range(r)]
    offsets = [0]
    for i in range(r-1):
        offsets.append(offsets[-1] ^ constants[i])
    potentials = [[tables[i][u][0] ^ constants[i] ^ offsets[i] for u in range(sizes[i])] for i in range(r)]
    return {'status':'lifted', 'potentials':potentials}
print(json.dumps([lift(item) for item in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A graph-analysis component can expose the identification decision under the stated input and refinement conventions. The paper supplies an exhaustive decision procedure and a worst-case complexity classification; deployed software is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The bijective game can be simulated to explore partial-isomorphism preservation and the correspondence with refinement. A finite observed play does not establish a winning strategy against every sequence of choices. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | An exact verification tool can use the exhaustive procedure as a reference for identification decisions within a manageable input regime. The paper provides no practical scalability guarantee. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper explicitly gives an exponential-time decision procedure and proves that the input-dimension identification problem is EXPTIME-complete. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Common integer color identifiers avoid expanding recursive color expressions while representing refinement on tuple tables. This is a source-supported representation for the decision procedure, without an independent general-purpose data-structure result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Hardware mappings, accelerator kernels, and measured hardware performance are ABSENT. The source establishes a graph decision procedure and complexity classification. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Cryptographic primitives, security models, and security reductions are ABSENT. Worst-case identification hardness alone supplies no cryptographic security contract. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Training procedures, statistical guarantees, and learned graph representations are ABSENT. The refinement convention is defined for exact graph identification. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Optimisation objectives, approximation guarantees, and operations-research algorithms are ABSENT. The source decides a threshold for identification dimension. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Distributed communication models, protocols, and communication bounds are ABSENT. The stated algorithm operates on an explicitly supplied adjacency matrix. |
| other (Graph identification and computational complexity) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper provides a complexity classification for graph identification and distinguishes identification from pair equivalence. Its reductions must account for every equivalent mate. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The identification decision assumes a nonempty finite simple undirected uncolored graph in adjacency-matrix form and a positive binary-encoded dimension. Blocked use: ['Applying the complexity classification to colored, directed, nonsimple, infinite or empty graph inputs.', 'Accepting nonpositive dimensions or unspecified graph encodings.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The refinement convention is part of the theorem: ordinary neighbor color refinement at dimension one and the joint-update rule at higher dimensions. Blocked use: ['Transferring the theorem to a refinement rule with separately counted coordinate multisets.']
- **L3** (counterexample_scope): Identification quantifies over every comparison graph, so deciding equivalence of a supplied pair is a different task. Blocked use: ['Using the identification theorem as the complexity classification of supplied-pair equivalence.', 'Treating one equivalent isomorphic mate as a certificate of identification.']
- **L4** (formal_scope): The theorem classifies the identification threshold decision; it does not supply refinement-round bounds or a runtime benchmark for a particular implementation. Blocked use: ['Inferring practical refinement runtime or round complexity directly from the identification classification.']
- **L5** (edge_case): Malformed encodings and nonpositive parameters are rejected, rather than interpreted as instances of the identification theorem. Blocked use: ['Assigning an identification answer to malformed encodings.']
- **L6** (edge_case): The upper-bound procedure accepts when the dimension is at least the graph order before constructing any tuple table. Blocked use: ['Expanding the tuple table for an arbitrarily large encoded dimension before checking graph order.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The game characterization assumes equal positive graph order and uses the stated shift between tuple dimension and pair slots. Blocked use: ['Equating tuple dimension directly with the same number of pair slots.', 'Applying the positive-order game statement without checking its graph-order hypotheses.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Reconstruction of every equivalent mate applies to the specified comparison construction with its dimension lower bound. Blocked use: ['Claiming that equivalent mates of an arbitrary graph have the shifted-square representation.']
- **L9** (counterexample_scope): Scalar image consistency concerns one comparison at a time and does not select compatible vectors across all comparisons. Blocked use: ['Replacing image-set equality with an asserted simultaneous pointwise solution.', 'Inferring graph isomorphism from scalar image consistency alone.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The image-consistency converse is proved for twists concentrated in one row and at one end of each wire. Blocked use: ['Applying special-twist sufficiency to arbitrary address-dependent square shifts.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): The isomorphism criterion requires one global offset assignment shared by every block using a readout. Blocked use: ['Combining independently chosen local block solutions into an asserted global isomorphism.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): Cycle lifting requires nonempty coordinate factors, a cyclic length satisfying the lemma, and an identically vanishing sum on the whole product. Blocked use: ['Using sampled zero sums as evidence of the universal premise.', 'Applying cycle lifting to empty factors or shorter cycles.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): The hardness reduction uses a uniform product-circuit compilation theorem from the companion paper; that compiler is a dependency of the construction. Blocked use: ['Presenting the computation compiler as an independently verified implementation supplied by this paper.']
- **L14** (counterexample_scope): Unary encoding of the positive dimension preserves the identification hardness classification. Blocked use: ['Claiming that unary encoding removes the hardness of the identification decision.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 133 (`lean/docs/133.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Parity lifts and bounded-treewidth witnesses for Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence](../../preprints/Parity-lifts-and-bounded-treewidth-witnesses-for-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [The complexity of identifying a graph by Weisfeiler–Leman refinement](../../preprints/The-complexity-of-identifying-a-graph-by-Weisfeiler-Leman-refinement-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Unconditional time lower bounds for Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence](../../preprints/Unconditional-time-lower-bounds-for-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Variable-dimension Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence on general and subcubic graphs](../../preprints/Variable-dimension-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-on-general-and-subcubic-graphs-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives the paper's parity-lift graph construction for every Weisfeiler–Leman dimension $k\ge4$, under both joint and separate conventions. From a finite choice system it constructs two equal-size uncolored graphs that are Weisfeiler–Leman equivalent exactly when the system has no successful compatible choice. The same equivalence holds for the pure variant.
>
> A successful choice is detected by the specified early round: round two in the joint convention and round one in the separate convention. The graph-size bound is linear in the choice-system size with a factor depending on $k$. The paper's conditional running-time exclusions are outside these selected construction statements.
>
> The formalization proves that deciding whether Weisfeiler–Leman refinement of a supplied dimension identifies a graph is EXPTIME-complete under polynomial-time many-one reductions. The input is a nonempty finite simple uncolored graph encoded by its adjacency matrix together with a positive binary-encoded dimension. Identification quantifies over every comparison graph, and the statement includes both the exponential-time upper bound and hardness.
>
> The formalization proves an unconditional deterministic time lower bound for deciding $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence. For every sufficiently large fixed $k$, every deciding algorithm in the stated multitape Turing-machine or sequential logarithmic-word RAM model requires at least $n^{ck}$ worst-case time for all sufficiently large graph orders $n$, for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The bound covers both joint and separate replacement conventions and remains valid for explicit adjacency-matrix inputs restricted to simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two.
>
> The formalization proves EXPTIME-completeness of deciding joint-update $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence when $k\ge2$ is part of the input in binary and the graphs are encoded by explicit adjacency matrices. It proves both the general result and the restriction to connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three. Completeness uses polynomial-time many-one reductions.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace WLIdentification

structure Graph where
  order : ℕ
  edge : Fin order → Fin order → Bool
  symmetric : ∀ u v, edge u v = edge v u
  loopless : ∀ u, edge u u = false

def Isomorphic (G H : Graph) : Prop :=
  ∃ e : Fin G.order ≃ Fin H.order, ∀ u v, G.edge u v = H.edge (e u) (e v)

/-- Colors retain their full history, and hence are shared between graphs. -/
def NeighborColor : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Unit
  | s + 1 => NeighborColor s × Multiset (NeighborColor s)

def neighborColor (G : Graph) : (s : ℕ) → Fin G.order → NeighborColor s
  | 0, _ => ()
  | s + 1, v => (neighborColor G s v,
      (Finset.univ.filter (fun w => G.edge v w = true)).val.map (neighborColor G s))

def neighborHistogram (G : Graph) (s : ℕ) : Multiset (NeighborColor s) :=
  Finset.univ.val.map (neighborColor G s)

def TupleColor (k : ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => (Fin k → Fin k → Bool) × (Fin k → Fin k → Bool)
  | s + 1 => TupleColor k s × Multiset (Fin k → TupleColor k s)

/-- Joint update: one multiset of whole replacement vectors, not independent marginals. -/
def tupleColor (G : Graph) (k : ℕ) : (s : ℕ) → (Fin k → Fin G.order) → TupleColor k s
  | 0, v => (fun i j => decide (v i = v j), fun i j => G.edge (v i) (v j))
  | s + 1, v => (tupleColor G k s v,
      Finset.univ.val.map (fun z => fun i => tupleColor G k s (Function.update v i z)))

def tupleHistogram (G : Graph) (k s : ℕ) : Multiset (TupleColor k s) :=
  Finset.univ.val.map (tupleColor G k s)

def Equivalent (k : ℕ) (G H : Graph) : Prop :=
  if k = 1 then ∀ s, neighborHistogram G s = neighborHistogram H s
  else ∀ s, tupleHistogram G k s = tupleHistogram H k s

/-- There is no restriction to mates from a graph construction or to a selected order. -/
def Identifies (k : ℕ) (G : Graph) : Prop :=
  ∀ H : Graph, Equivalent k G H → Isomorphic G H

/-- The infimum of the positive identifying dimensions; this set is nonempty for finite G. -/
noncomputable def WLdim (G : Graph) : ℕ :=
  sInf {k : ℕ | 0 < k ∧ Identifies k G}

abbrev Word := List Bool
abbrev BinaryLanguage := Set Word

def binaryValue (bits : Word) : ℕ :=
  bits.foldl (fun z b => 2 * z + if b then 1 else 0) 0

def matrixWord (G : Graph) : Word :=
  (List.ofFn (fun i : Fin G.order => List.ofFn (G.edge i))).flatten

/-- Unary order header, row-major binary matrix, then most-significant-first binary k. -/
def inputWord (G : Graph) (bits : Word) : Word :=
  List.replicate G.order true ++ [false] ++ matrixWord G ++ bits

/-- A malformed word is rejected: membership requires a valid nonempty simple matrix and k>0. -/
def dimensionLanguage : BinaryLanguage :=
  {w | ∃ (G : Graph) (bits : Word),
    0 < G.order ∧ (∃ tail, bits = true :: tail) ∧ 0 < binaryValue bits ∧
    w = inputWord G bits ∧ WLdim G ≤ binaryValue bits}

/- The deterministic local machine model is explicit; no oracle or arbitrary
   state-transition on whole inputs is included. Both alphabets are fixed finite
   types for each machine. Extra symbols and states allow arbitrary finite tables. -/
inductive Move where
  | left | stay | right
  deriving DecidableEq

def Move.displacement : Move → ℤ
  | .left => -1
  | .stay => 0
  | .right => 1

structure Machine where
  extraSymbols : ℕ
  extraStates : ℕ
  transition : Fin (extraStates + 3) → Fin (extraSymbols + 3) →
    Fin (extraStates + 3) × Fin (extraSymbols + 3) × Move

structure Configuration (M : Machine) where
  state : Fin (M.extraStates + 3)
  head : ℤ
  tape : ℤ → Fin (M.extraSymbols + 3)

def inputSymbol (M : Machine) (b : Bool) : Fin (M.extraSymbols + 3) :=
  if b then 2 else 1

def initial (M : Machine) (w : Word) : Configuration M where
  state := 0
  head := 0
  tape z := if 0 ≤ z then (w[z.toNat]?).elim 0 (inputSymbol M) else 0

def Halted {M : Machine} (c : Configuration M) : Prop :=
  c.state = 1 ∨ c.state = 2

/-- One read/write/head-movement transition. A halted configuration is frozen. -/
def step (M : Machine) (c : Configuration M) : Configuration M :=
  if c.state = 1 ∨ c.state = 2 then c else
    let t := M.transition c.state (c.tape c.head)
    { state := t.1, head := c.head + t.2.2.displacement,
      tape := Function.update c.tape c.head t.2.1 }

def run (M : Machine) (w : Word) (t : ℕ) : Configuration M :=
  (step M)^[t] (initial M w)

/-- Output is the binary word beginning at origin and ending at its first blank. -/
def Output {M : Machine} (c : Configuration M) (w : Word) : Prop :=
  (∀ i : Fin w.length, c.tape (i.val : ℤ) = inputSymbol M w[i]) ∧
    c.tape (w.length : ℤ) = 0

/-- Uniform genuine executions on every bit word, including malformed graph words. -/
def DecidesWithin (M : Machine) (L : BinaryLanguage) (bound : ℕ → ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ w, ∃ t ≤ bound w.length, Halted (run M w t) ∧ ((run M w t).state = 1 ↔ w ∈ L)

def InEXPTIME (L : BinaryLanguage) : Prop :=
  ∃ (M : Machine) (C d : ℕ),
    DecidesWithin M L (fun n => 2 ^ (C * (n + 1) ^ d))

def ComputesWithin (M : Machine) (f : Word → Word) (bound : ℕ → ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ w, ∃ t ≤ bound w.length, (run M w t).state = 1 ∧ Output (run M w t) (f w)

def PolytimeManyOne (L S : BinaryLanguage) : Prop :=
  ∃ (f : Word → Word) (M : Machine) (C d : ℕ),
    ComputesWithin M f (fun n => C * (n + 1) ^ d) ∧ ∀ w, w ∈ L ↔ f w ∈ S

def EXPTIMEComplete (S : BinaryLanguage) : Prop :=
  InEXPTIME S ∧ ∀ L : BinaryLanguage, InEXPTIME L → PolytimeManyOne L S

theorem main : EXPTIMEComplete dimensionLanguage :=
  by
    sorry

end WLIdentification

end OAI
```

Identification quantifies over every comparison graph, and the statement includes both the exponential-time upper bound and hardness.\n\nThe formalization proves an unconditional deterministic time lower bound for deciding $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence. For every sufficiently large fixed $k$, every deciding algorithm in the stated multitape Turing-machine or sequential logarithmic-word RAM model requires at least $n^{ck}$ worst-case time for all sufficiently large graph orders $n$, for one absolute $c>0$.\n\nThe bound covers both joint and separate replacement conventions and remains valid for explicit adjacency-matrix inputs restricted to simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two.\n\nThe formalization proves EXPTIME-completeness of deciding joint-update $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence when $k\\ge2$ is part of the input in binary and the graphs are encoded by explicit adjacency matrices. It proves both the general result and the restriction to connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three. Completeness uses polynomial-time many-one reductions."
  },
  "status": "present",
  "toolchain": "leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1"
}

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that deciding whether Weisfeiler–Leman refinement of an input dimension identifies a given graph is EXPTIME-complete. The input is a nonempty finite simple uncolored graph in adjacency-matrix form and a positive binary-encoded dimension. Identification quantifies over every comparison graph.
